It’s more like I get pulled into it, and then I realize everybody is staring or yelling, and I’m still stuck for a bit before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: While you’re doing these things—like copying the beeping sounds, repeating gestures, or arranging the boxes—do you ever feel like part of you is trying to stop, or do you feel like you’re completely caught up in it without any part of you recognizing that it’s not right?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of both, I think. I’m not completely gone in the sense that I have no idea it’s happening. Like, if I’m by the forklift and my arms are up, or I’m making that beeping sound back at it, there is usually some part of me that knows, like, this is not what I’m supposed to be doing. I can hear people yelling, and I know I’m holding things up.\n\nBut knowing it and stopping it don’t happen at the same time. That’s the part that scares me a little, because I’ll be thinking, okay, stop, put your arms down, quit making the noise, just move away… and then I’m still doing it for a few more seconds, or longer. Same with the hand signals. I’ll see someone gesture, and I’m doing it back, and I know nobody asked me to do that, but it’s like I get pulled into copying it before I can catch myself.\n\nWith the boxes, it’s maybe even harder, because I’ll start moving them into those little patterns, and I can tell it looks strange and people are mad, but I get stuck trying to make them look… I don’t know, lined up in the way my head wants them right then. So there is a part of me trying to stop, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m totally unaware. It’s more like I’m aware enough to feel embarrassed, but not in control enough to quit right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have no control over these behaviors when they start, or do you always feel like part of you knows you could stop if you really tried?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it feels like I could just stop if I really tried, no. I mean, I know that sounds like an excuse, and that’s part of why I feel bad saying it, because at the warehouse it probably looks like I’m just messing around or not listening. But when it starts, it really does feel like I don’t have much control over it right away.\n\nLike with the forklift, if it’s backing up and the beeping starts, I’ll get stuck there, sometimes with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back. And I can hear people yelling, like, telling me to move or knock it off, and I know they’re not wrong. I know I’m in the way. But it’s like my body is already doing the thing and I’m trying to catch up to it.\n\nSame with the hand signals or the boxes. I’ll see a gesture and repeat it, or I’ll start moving boxes into these odd little patterns, and even if everyone’s getting mad, I can’t just snap out of it instantly. There might be a part of me that knows I should stop, but it doesn’t feel like that part is in charge. It’s more like I have to wait until the pull lets up enough for me to actually stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice any strange sensations — like something in your vision, a sound in your ears, or a feeling in your body — right before you start doing the movements or get pulled into arranging the boxes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed something specific like that. Not like a flash in my vision or a ringing sound or anything I could point to and say, “That happens first, then I do it.” It’s hard, because when it starts, I’m usually already kind of in the middle of it before I realize I’m doing it.\n\nWith the forklift thing, I mostly notice the beeping itself, obviously, but I don’t know if that counts as a strange sound in my ears or just the actual forklift making noise. It’s more like the sound catches me, and then I’m stuck on it. Same with the boxes — I don’t think I feel some warning in my body first. I just start seeing how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them around before I’ve really decided to.\n\nMaybe there’s a kind of tension or pressure, but I don’t know if I’m making that up now because you asked. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I haven’t been paying attention to what happens right before, because usually I only notice once someone yells or once I realize I’ve been standing there doing it. So… no, not really any clear strange sensation that I can remember.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve heard people describe that kind of thing, like thoughts being inserted or pulled out, and I don’t really know how I would even tell that was happening. My thoughts feel like they’re in my own head, I guess, even when they’re annoying or hard to slow down.

Like, I don’t feel this clear thing building up in me where I’m like, “I have to make the sound now” or “I have to move my arms.” It’s not that neat.\n\nIt’s more like I’m already doing it before I really catch it. I’ll be standing near the forklift and then I’ve got my arms up, kind of stuck there, or I’m doing the beep-beep sound it makes, and I don’t remember deciding, okay, do that. I know they’re supposed to go where the freight goes, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, but in the moment it just feels like that’s what I’m doing, like I’m locked into it. It’s not like I see someone pushing my arms or anything. But it also doesn’t feel like normal thoughts, like I’m planning it out.\n\nAt the warehouse it’s more like I just get caught in it. I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just standing there with my arms up, sort of frozen, and I didn’t really decide, “Okay, put your arms up now.” Or I hear the forklift beeping and then I’m doing the same beep-beep thing back, like copying it, and then I keep doing it until someone snaps at me. Same with the hand signals the guys use — I’ll repeat them, like my hands just go into it.\n\nAnd the boxes… that’s the part they get the most mad about, because I start moving them into these little patterns, like they line up a certain way in my head, but I know that’s not how we’re supposed to stack freight. People are yelling, “Stop doing that, you’re holding us up,” and I’m trying to stop, but I’m already in the middle of it.\n\nSo… I guess it feels like it’s coming from me, but not in a way I’m fully choosing. Not like a clear outside force, more like I’m pulled into it and then I realize everyone is staring.\n\nDoctor: Do these movements, sounds, or patterns happen only at work, or do they also happen when you're at home or in other places?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work, yeah. I mean, that’s where people notice it and where it gets me in trouble, because everything is moving fast there and I’m supposed to be stacking freight or moving boxes, not… standing there.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and I sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sound it makes — that little backing-up beep — and I’ll do it over and over, or I’ll copy the hand signals the guys use. And then with the boxes, I keep moving them into these weird little patterns, like they have to sit a certain way, even though everybody’s yelling at me because I’m messing up the flow and holding things up.\n\nThat’s kind of why they brought me in, I guess. Like… strange thoughts, everybody has strange thoughts sometimes, right? I get thoughts that feel kind of weird or hard to explain, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nAs far as voices, I don’t think I hear, like, a person standing there talking when nobody’s there. Not like in a movie or something. I’ll get caught up in noises around me, like the beeping at work, but I don’t know if that counts, because it’s a real sound first. It’s harder to say, because at home nobody’s really watching me the same way, you know? Like if I pause or move something around, it doesn’t turn into everybody yelling.\n\nBut the big problem, the thing that made them bring me in, is definitely at the warehouse. I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll just kind of freeze there with my arms up, like I’m stuck in the middle of doing something, and I don’t really mean to stand like that. Or the forklift starts backing up and beeping, and then I catch myself making the same beep sound back, over and over. And the hand signals the guys use, I’ll copy those too, even when there’s no reason to.\n\nThe boxes are another one. I keep moving them into these patterns, like they look wrong unless I shift them a certain way, even though I know I’m supposed to just stack them and keep things moving. People will be yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, and I still keep messing with them for a bit before I can pull myself out of it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it a “need” exactly, but yeah, it’s like I get pulled into doing things over and over even when I don’t really want to, mostly at work.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or arranging the boxes, do you ever notice any specific feeling, sound, or thought that comes right before it happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t always catch a clear thing right before it. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, because people ask like there must be a thought first, like, “I’m going to do this now,” but it doesn’t really feel that organized to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, it’s like I’ll be near the forklift, and then suddenly I’m just… stopped, with my arms up, like I froze in the middle of something. Or the forklift starts doing that backing-up beep, and I notice the sound and then I’m making it too, like copying it, and I keep doing it longer than makes sense. Same with the hand signals the guys use — I’ll see them and then I’m doing them back, even if nobody asked me to.\n\nWith the boxes, I guess sometimes they just look wrong where they are. Not wrong like dangerous, just wrong, like the little pattern of them is off, and then I start shifting them around into these strange arrangements. And I know people are getting mad, because they’re yelling at me to stop and keep moving, but I still get stuck doing it for a bit.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s a feeling exactly. Sometimes it’s a sound, like the beeping, or seeing a gesture, or just the boxes looking like they need to be a certain way. But a lot of the time I only realize it after I’m already doing it and everybody is reacting.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or feel pulled into those patterns, do you ever notice any strange feelings in your body, like a tingling, pressure, or something else unusual, even for a second?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like tingling or pressure. I’m trying to check in my memory, but it’s hard because when it happens I’m usually not paying attention to my body first. I’m paying attention to the thing in front of me, or I’m already doing it before I realize.\n\nLike with the beeping, I notice the sound more than anything inside me. With the boxes, it’s more like my eyes catch on how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them. I can’t really say there’s a feeling in my hands or my chest or my head that comes first. Maybe sometimes I get tense, but I don’t know if that’s before or after people start yelling or after I realize I’m stuck doing it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed a specific body feeling right before. If there is one, it’s not obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like these behaviors—like copying sounds, repeating gestures, or arranging boxes—don’t make sense or that you shouldn’t be doing them, even if you still feel pulled to do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I do know it doesn’t really make sense. That’s what makes it embarrassing, honestly. I’m not standing there thinking, “This is a good idea, I should copy the forklift,” or “These boxes need to be in some special design.” I know I’m supposed to be working, moving stuff, staying out of the way, doing what the guys are telling me.\n\nBut then I get caught in it anyway. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start making the same beeping sound back when it’s backing up. And even while I’m doing it, part of me knows it looks weird and that I should stop. Same with the hand signals — I’ll copy them even when nobody is signaling me. And the boxes, I’ll start shifting them into these little patterns, and people are yelling at me to cut it out, and I know they’re mad because I’m slowing everything down.\n\nSo yeah, I know I shouldn’t be doing it. It’s not like it makes sense to me afterward. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and then I realize everybody is staring or yelling, and I’m still stuck for a bit before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: While you’re doing these things—like copying the beeping sounds, repeating gestures, or arranging the boxes—do you ever feel like part of you is trying to stop, or do you feel like you’re completely caught up in it without any part of you recognizing that it’s not right?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of both, I think. I’m not completely gone in the sense that I have no idea it’s happening. Like, if I’m by the forklift and my arms are up, or I’m making that beeping sound back at it, there is usually some part of me that knows, like, this is not what I’m supposed to be doing. I can hear people yelling, and I know I’m holding things up.\n\nBut knowing it and stopping it don’t happen at the same time. That’s the part that scares me a little, because I’ll be thinking, okay, stop, put your arms down, quit making the noise, just move away… and then I’m still doing it for a few more seconds, or longer. Same with the hand signals. I’ll see someone gesture, and I’m doing it back, and I know nobody asked me to do that, but it’s like I get pulled into copying it before I can catch myself.\n\nWith the boxes, it’s maybe even harder, because I’ll start moving them into those little patterns, and I can tell it looks strange and people are mad, but I get stuck trying to make them look… I don’t know, lined up in the way my head wants them right then. So there is a part of me trying to stop, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m totally unaware. It’s more like I’m aware enough to feel embarrassed, but not in control enough to quit right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have no control over these behaviors when they start, or do you always feel like part of you knows you could stop if you really tried?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it feels like I could just stop if I really tried, no. I mean, I know that sounds like an excuse, and that’s part of why I feel bad saying it, because at the warehouse it probably looks like I’m just messing around or not listening. But when it starts, it really does feel like I don’t have much control over it right away.\n\nLike with the forklift, if it’s backing up and the beeping starts, I’ll get stuck there, sometimes with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back. And I can hear people yelling, like, telling me to move or knock it off, and I know they’re not wrong. I know I’m in the way. But it’s like my body is already doing the thing and I’m trying to catch up to it.\n\nSame with the hand signals or the boxes. I’ll see a gesture and repeat it, or I’ll start moving boxes into these odd little patterns, and even if everyone’s getting mad, I can’t just snap out of it instantly. There might be a part of me that knows I should stop, but it doesn’t feel like that part is in charge. It’s more like I have to wait until the pull lets up enough for me to actually stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice any strange sensations — like something in your vision, a sound in your ears, or a feeling in your body — right before you start doing the movements or get pulled into arranging the boxes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed something specific like that. Not like a flash in my vision or a ringing sound or anything I could point to and say, “That happens first, then I do it.” It’s hard, because when it starts, I’m usually already kind of in the middle of it before I realize I’m doing it.\n\nWith the forklift thing, I mostly notice the beeping itself, obviously, but I don’t know if that counts as a strange sound in my ears or just the actual forklift making noise. It’s more like the sound catches me, and then I’m stuck on it. Same with the boxes — I don’t think I feel some warning in my body first. I just start seeing how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them around before I’ve really decided to.\n\nMaybe there’s a kind of tension or pressure, but I don’t know if I’m making that up now because you asked. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I haven’t been paying attention to what happens right before, because usually I only notice once someone yells or once I realize I’ve been standing there doing it. So… no, not really any clear strange sensation that I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or like your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve heard people describe that kind of thing, like thoughts being inserted or pulled out, and I don’t really know how I would even tell that was happening. My thoughts feel like they’re in my own head, I guess, even when they’re annoying or hard to slow down.\n\nI don’t have a clear feeling like some other person is putting words in there, or like somebody reached in and removed something. And I don’t think I’ve felt like everyone can hear what I’m thinking, at least not in that literal way. Sometimes I worry people can tell I’m acting weird because of how I look or what I’m doing, but that’s more from me noticing their faces or them reacting, not because I think my thoughts are being broadcast like a radio or something.\n\nSo… no, not really. Not that I’ve noticed. If you mean something more subtle than that, I might need you to explain it, but the way you said it, I don’t think that’s happened to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

This supports narrowing away from brief psychotic disorder (F23.x) and toward a more persistent schizophrenia spectrum disorder (F25.x). No clear focal warning sensations (e.g., auras) were reported, making an epileptic or sensory-triggered explanation less likely.",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think there is sometimes a moment like that, but it’s very small, like I don’t get enough time to do anything with it.

Like at the warehouse, with the forklift, if it starts backing up and the beeping is going, I can sort of know, “Don’t do it, don’t get stuck here,” because I’ve already gotten in trouble for it. I know people are trying to work and I know it’s dangerous or annoying or whatever. But then I’ll freeze there with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back, and by the time I’m aware enough to think, “Stop, stop doing that,” it’s already happening.

It’s the same with the hand signals. Someone gives a signal and I’ll copy it, even if part of me is embarrassed and knows they’re going to yell. Or with the boxes, I don’t set out thinking, “I’m going to make these weird patterns and hold everyone up.” I just get pulled into moving them around into these little arrangements, and then I hear people getting mad, and that makes me realize I’m doing it again.

So yes, I guess I do sometimes know I don’t want to, but it doesn’t feel like that knowing is enough to stop it right then. It feels like I’m already caught in it.

I’m not completely gone in the sense that I have no idea it’s happening. Like, if I’m by the forklift and my arms are up, or I’m making that beeping sound back at it, there is usually some part of me that knows, like, this is not what I’m supposed to be doing. And I can hear people yelling, like, telling me to move or knock it off, and I know they’re not wrong. I know I’m in the way. But it’s like my body is already doing the thing and I’m trying to catch up to it.\n\nSame with the hand signals or the boxes. I’ll see a gesture and repeat it, or I’ll start moving boxes into these odd little patterns, and even if everyone’s getting mad, I can’t just snap out of it instantly. There might be a part of me that knows I should stop, but it doesn’t feel like that part is in charge. It’s more like I have to wait until the pull lets up enough for me to actually stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice any strange sensations — like something in your vision, a sound in your ears, or a feeling in your body — right before you start doing the movements or get pulled into arranging the boxes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed something specific like that. Not like a flash in my vision or a ringing sound or anything I could point to and say, “That happens first, then I do it.” It’s hard, because when it starts, I’m usually already kind of in the middle of it before I realize I’m doing it.\n\nWith the forklift thing, I mostly notice the beeping itself, obviously, but I don’t know if that counts as a strange sound in my ears or just the actual forklift making noise. It’s more like the sound catches me, and then I’m stuck on it. Same with the boxes — I don’t think I feel some warning in my body first. I just start seeing how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them around before I’ve really decided to.\n\nMaybe there’s a kind of tension or pressure, but I don’t know if I’m making that up now because you asked. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I haven’t been paying attention to what happens right before, because usually I only notice once someone yells or once I realize I’ve been standing there doing it. So… no, not really any clear strange sensation that I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or like your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve heard people describe that kind of thing, like thoughts being inserted or pulled out, and I don’t really know how I would even tell that was happening. My thoughts feel like they’re in my own head, I guess, even when they’re annoying or hard to slow down.\n\nI don’t have a clear feeling like some other person is putting words in there, or like somebody reached in and removed something. And I don’t think I’ve felt like everyone can hear what I’m thinking, at least not in that literal way. Sometimes I worry people can tell I’m acting weird because of how I look or what I’m doing, but that’s more from me noticing their faces or them reacting, not because I think my thoughts are being broadcast like a radio or something.\n\nSo… no, not really. Not that I’ve noticed. If you mean something more subtle than that, I might need you to explain it, but the way you said it, I don’t think that’s happened to me.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or arranging the boxes, do you ever have a moment where you know you don’t want to do it but feel like you can’t stop it from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there is sometimes a moment like that, but it’s very small, like I don’t get enough time to do anything with it.\n\nLike at the warehouse, with the forklift, if it starts backing up and the beeping is going, I can sort of know, “Don’t do it, don’t get stuck here,” because I’ve already gotten in trouble for it. I know people are trying to work and I know it’s dangerous or annoying or whatever. But then I’ll freeze there with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back, and by the time I’m aware enough to think, “Stop, stop doing that,” it’s already happening.\n\nIt’s the same with the hand signals. Someone gives a signal and I’ll copy it, even if part of me is embarrassed and knows they’re going to yell. Or with the boxes, I don’t set out thinking, “I’m going to make these weird patterns and hold everyone up.” I just get pulled into moving them around into these little arrangements, and then I hear people getting mad, and that makes me realize I’m doing it again.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do sometimes know I don’t want to, but it doesn’t feel like that knowing is enough to stop it right then. It feels like I’m already caught in it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, sometimes I think there is a tiny moment, but it’s not clear like a warning bell. It’s more like I suddenly realize, “Oh no, this is the thing again,” and then it’s already happening.

Like with the forklift at the warehouse, if it starts beeping or someone is giving hand signals, I’ll sort of feel myself getting caught on it. I might freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sound, or I copy the gesture, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m in the way or I’m making it worse. But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.

And with the boxes, it’s similar. I don’t plan it out ahead of time. I just see them sitting there, and then I’m moving them into these weird little patterns, like they need to be arranged a certain way, even though everyone else just wants me to keep working. I guess that’s part of why they brought me in, because I keep doing that stuff even when people are upset and telling me to stop.

So… yes, maybe there’s a brief moment, but it’s not useful. It doesn’t feel like enough time to stop myself.

Or with the boxes, I don’t set out thinking, “I’m going to make these weird patterns and hold everyone up.” I just get pulled into moving them around into these little arrangements, and then I hear people getting mad, and that makes me realize I’m doing it again.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do sometimes know I don’t want to, but it doesn’t feel like that knowing is enough to stop it right then. It feels like I’m already caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a brief moment before you start doing these things—like copying sounds, gestures, or moving boxes—where you have a clear feeling that it’s about to happen, even if you can’t stop it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I think there is a tiny moment, but it’s not clear like a warning bell. It’s more like I suddenly realize, “Oh no, this is the thing again,” and then it’s already happening.\n\nLike with the forklift at the warehouse, if it starts beeping or someone is giving hand signals, I’ll sort of feel myself getting caught on it. I might freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sound, or I copy the gesture, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m in the way or I’m making it worse. But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.\n\nAnd with the boxes, it’s similar. I don’t plan it out ahead of time. I just see them sitting there, and then I’m moving them into these weird little patterns, like they need to be arranged a certain way, even though everyone else just wants me to keep working. I guess that’s part of why they brought me in, because I keep doing that stuff even when people are upset and telling me to stop.\n\nSo… yes, maybe there’s a brief moment, but it’s not useful. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call it relief exactly. It’s not like I do the thing and then feel calm in a good way, like, “Okay, that fixed it.” Sometimes there’s maybe a tiny feeling that it’s… finished, or like my body stops pulling at me for a second, but mostly I just notice everyone reacting.

Like at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I copy the beeping back, or copy the hand signals, I’m not doing it because I think, “This will feel satisfying.” It just happens, and then after it’s done I’m usually aware that people are yelling or staring, and I feel stupid or embarrassed or scared I’m going to get in more trouble.

With the boxes, maybe there’s a little more of that settled feeling, because when I move them into those strange little patterns, part of me feels like they’re in the right place for a moment. But then someone comes over and says, “What are you doing, stop messing with those,” and then that feeling goes away and I just feel caught, like I did it again and I don’t really know how to explain why.

So… maybe for the boxes, yes, a small moment of “that’s how it should be,” but with the sounds and movements it’s more like it ends and then I have to deal with everyone being mad.

But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.\n\nAnd with the boxes, it’s similar. I don’t plan it out ahead of time. I just see them sitting there, and then I’m moving them into these weird little patterns, like they need to be arranged a certain way, even though everyone else just wants me to keep working. I guess that’s part of why they brought me in, because I keep doing that stuff even when people are upset and telling me to stop.\n\nSo… yes, maybe there’s a brief moment, but it’s not useful. It doesn’t feel like enough time to stop myself.\n\nDoctor: After you finish doing the movements, copying the sounds, or arranging the boxes, do you ever feel a sense of relief, satisfaction, or like something is settled, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it relief exactly. It’s not like I do the thing and then feel calm in a good way, like, “Okay, that fixed it.” Sometimes there’s maybe a tiny feeling that it’s… finished, or like my body stops pulling at me for a second, but mostly I just notice everyone reacting.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I copy the beeping back, or copy the hand signals, I’m not doing it because I think, “This will feel satisfying.” It just happens, and then after it’s done I’m usually aware that people are yelling or staring, and I feel stupid or embarrassed or scared I’m going to get in more trouble.\n\nWith the boxes, maybe there’s a little more of that settled feeling, because when I move them into those strange little patterns, part of me feels like they’re in the right place for a moment. But then someone comes over and says, “What are you doing, stop messing with those,” and then that feeling goes away and I just feel caught, like I did it again and I don’t really know how to explain why.\n\nSo… maybe for the boxes, yes, a small moment of “that’s how it should be,” but with the sounds and movements it’s more like it ends and then I have to deal with everyone being mad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s more like I suddenly realize, “Oh no, this is the thing again,” and then it’s already happening.\n\nLike with the forklift at the warehouse, if it starts beeping or someone is giving hand signals, I’ll sort of feel myself getting caught on it. I might freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sound, or I copy the gesture, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m in the way or I’m making it worse. But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.\n\nAnd with the boxes, it’s similar. I don’t plan it out ahead of time. I just see them sitting there, and then I’m moving them into these weird little patterns, like they need to be arranged a certain way, even though everyone else just wants me to keep working. I guess that’s part of why they brought me in, because I keep doing that stuff even when people are upset and telling me to stop.\n\nSo… yes, maybe there’s a brief moment, but it’s not useful. It doesn’t feel like enough time to stop myself.\n\nDoctor: After you finish doing the movements, copying the sounds, or arranging the boxes, do you ever feel a sense of relief, satisfaction, or like something is settled, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it relief exactly. It’s not like I do the thing and then feel calm in a good way, like, “Okay, that fixed it.” Sometimes there’s maybe a tiny feeling that it’s… finished, or like my body stops pulling at me for a second, but mostly I just notice everyone reacting.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I copy the beeping back, or copy the hand signals, I’m not doing it because I think, “This will feel satisfying.” It just happens, and then after it’s done I’m usually aware that people are yelling or staring, and I feel stupid or embarrassed or scared I’m going to get in more trouble.\n\nWith the boxes, maybe there’s a little more of that settled feeling, because when I move them into those strange little patterns, part of me feels like they’re in the right place for a moment. But then someone comes over and says, “What are you doing, stop messing with those,” and then that feeling goes away and I just feel caught, like I did it again and I don’t really know how to explain why.\n\nSo… maybe for the boxes, yes, a small moment of “that’s how it should be,” but with the sounds and movements it’s more like it ends and then I have to deal with everyone being mad.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore you start doing the movements, copying sounds, or arranging the boxes, do you ever feel a subtle inner pressure, discomfort, or need building up inside that makes you feel like you have to do it, even if only for a moment?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Yeah… I think there is something like that, but it’s hard to separate it out from the thing itself. It’s not like I’m standing there calmly and then I feel this big pressure building for a long time. It’s more like I’m at the warehouse and something catches me, like the forklift starts beeping, or somebody makes one of those hand signals, and suddenly there’s this tight feeling, like my body has already decided before I have.

That’s why I guess they brought me in, because I keep doing these things even when it makes no sense. But in that moment it feels like there’s this need or pull, not exactly in words, just like I have to follow it.

With the boxes it’s maybe easier to notice. I’ll see them sitting there and then there’s this uncomfortable feeling that they’re not right, like they need to be moved into these little patterns. So I start arranging them, even if everyone is trying to work around me and getting mad. I know it looks strange. I know it holds things up. But it’s like the pressure is there and I’m already doing it before I can talk myself out of it.

I mean, I’ve heard people describe that kind of thing, like thoughts being inserted or pulled out, and I don’t really know how I would even tell that was happening. My thoughts feel like they’re in my own head, I guess, even when they’re annoying or hard to slow down.\n\nI don’t have a clear feeling like some other person is putting words in there, or like somebody reached in and removed something. Sometimes I worry people can tell I’m acting weird because of how I look or what I’m doing, but that’s more from me noticing their faces or them reacting, not because I think my thoughts are being broadcast like a radio or something.\n\nSo… no, not really. Not that I’ve noticed. If you mean something more subtle than that, I might need you to explain it, but the way you said it, I don’t think that’s happened to me.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or arranging the boxes, do you ever have a moment where you know you don’t want to do it but feel like you can’t stop it from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there is sometimes a moment like that, but it’s very small, like I don’t get enough time to do anything with it.\n\nLike at the warehouse, with the forklift, if it starts backing up and the beeping is going, I can sort of know, “Don’t do it, don’t get stuck here,” because I’ve already gotten in trouble for it. I know people are trying to work and I know it’s dangerous or annoying or whatever. But then I’ll freeze there with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back, and by the time I’m aware enough to think, “Stop, stop doing that,” it’s already happening.\n\nIt’s the same with the hand signals. Someone gives a signal and I’ll copy it, even if part of me is embarrassed and knows they’re going to yell. It feels like I’m already caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a brief moment before you start doing these things—like copying sounds, gestures, or moving boxes—where you have a clear feeling that it’s about to happen, even if you can’t stop it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I think there is a tiny moment, but it’s not clear like a warning bell. I might freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sound, or I copy the gesture, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m in the way or I’m making it worse. But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.\n\nAnd with the boxes, it’s similar. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… a lot of the time it does feel like that. Not like I black out or anything, I’m there, I can see what’s happening, but it’s like my body gets ahead of me.

Like at the warehouse, by the forklift, I’ll suddenly be standing there with my arms up, frozen in that position, or I’ll start doing the same hand signal somebody else is doing. And I know it looks ridiculous or dangerous, because people are yelling at me to move or stop, but in that moment it doesn’t feel like I sat there and chose, “Okay, now I’m going to raise my arms.” It feels like it just… starts.

Same with the beeping sounds. The forklift beeps, and then I’m copying it back before I really mean to. I hear myself doing it, and then I realize people are staring or getting mad. It’s embarrassing because I’m not trying to mess around or be funny. I know it causes problems.

And with the boxes, I guess that’s a little different because it’s more like I get stuck moving them into these weird little patterns. But even that doesn’t feel fully under my control. I’ll know I’m supposed to be working normally, and people are telling me to stop, but I keep doing it anyway, like my hands are already going.

So, yeah… I’d say it feels out of my control, or mostly out of my control. I’m aware of it, but I don’t feel like I’m the one steering it very well.

I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll just kind of freeze there with my arms up, like I’m stuck in the middle of doing something, and I don’t really mean to stand like that. With the boxes, it’s more like my eyes catch on how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them. I can’t really say there’s a feeling in my hands or my chest or my head that comes first. Maybe sometimes I get tense, but I don’t know if that’s before or after people start yelling or after I realize I’m stuck doing it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed a specific body feeling right before. If there is one, it’s not obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like these behaviors—like copying sounds, repeating gestures, or arranging boxes—don’t make sense or that you shouldn’t be doing them, even if you still feel pulled to do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I do know it doesn’t really make sense. That’s what makes it embarrassing, honestly. I’m not standing there thinking, “This is a good idea, I should copy the forklift,” or “These boxes need to be in some special design.” I know I’m supposed to be working, moving stuff, staying out of the way, doing what the guys are telling me.\n\nBut then I get caught in it anyway. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll start making the same beeping sound back when it’s backing up. And even while I’m doing it, part of me knows it looks weird and that I should stop. Same with the hand signals — I’ll copy them even when nobody is signaling me. And the boxes, I’ll start shifting them into these little patterns, and people are yelling at me to cut it out, and I know they’re mad because I’m slowing everything down.\n\nSo yeah, I know I shouldn’t be doing it. It’s not like it makes sense to me afterward. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and then I realize everybody is staring or yelling, and I’m still stuck for a bit before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: While you’re doing these things—like copying the beeping sounds, repeating gestures, or arranging the boxes—do you ever feel like part of you is trying to stop, or do you feel like you’re completely caught up in it without any part of you recognizing that it’s not right?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of both, I think. I’m not completely gone in the sense that I have no idea it’s happening. Like, if I’m by the forklift and my arms are up, or I’m making that beeping sound back at it, there is usually some part of me that knows, like, this is not what I’m supposed to be doing. I can hear people yelling, and I know I’m holding things up.\n\nBut knowing it and stopping it don’t happen at the same time. That’s the part that scares me a little, because I’ll be thinking, okay, stop, put your arms down, quit making the noise, just move away… and then I’m still doing it for a few more seconds, or longer. Same with the hand signals. I’ll see someone gesture, and I’m doing it back, and I know nobody asked me to do that, but it’s like I get pulled into copying it before I can catch myself.\n\nWith the boxes, it’s maybe even harder, because I’ll start moving them into those little patterns, and I can tell it looks strange and people are mad, but I get stuck trying to make them look… I don’t know, lined up in the way my head wants them right then. So there is a part of me trying to stop, yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m totally unaware. It’s more like I’m aware enough to feel embarrassed, but not in control enough to quit right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have no control over these behaviors when they start, or do you always feel like part of you knows you could stop if you really tried?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it feels like I could just stop if I really tried, no. I mean, I know that sounds like an excuse, and that’s part of why I feel bad saying it, because at the warehouse it probably looks like I’m just messing around or not listening. But when it starts, it really does feel like I don’t have much control over it right away.\n\nLike with the forklift, if it’s backing up and the beeping starts, I’ll get stuck there, sometimes with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back. I’ll see a gesture and repeat it, or I’ll start moving boxes into these odd little patterns, and even if everyone’s getting mad, I can’t just snap out of it instantly. There might be a part of me that knows I should stop, but it doesn’t feel like that part is in charge. It’s more like I have to wait until the pull lets up enough for me to actually stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice any strange sensations — like something in your vision, a sound in your ears, or a feeling in your body — right before you start doing the movements or get pulled into arranging the boxes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed something specific like that. Not like a flash in my vision or a ringing sound or anything I could point to and say, “That happens first, then I do it.” It’s hard, because when it starts, I’m usually already kind of in the middle of it before I realize I’m doing it.\n\nWith the forklift thing, I mostly notice the beeping itself, obviously, but I don’t know if that counts as a strange sound in my ears or just the actual forklift making noise. It’s more like the sound catches me, and then I’m stuck on it. Same with the boxes — I don’t think I feel some warning in my body first. I just start seeing how they’re sitting there, and then I’m moving them around before I’ve really decided to.\n\nMaybe there’s a kind of tension or pressure, but I don’t know if I’m making that up now because you asked. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I haven’t been paying attention to what happens right before, because usually I only notice once someone yells or once I realize I’ve been standing there doing it. So… no, not really any clear strange sensation that I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or like your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve heard people describe that kind of thing, like thoughts being inserted or pulled out, and I don’t really know how I would even tell that was happening. My thoughts feel like they’re in my own head, I guess, even when they’re annoying or hard to slow down.\n\nI don’t have a clear feeling like some other person is putting words in there, or like somebody reached in and removed something. And I don’t think I’ve felt like everyone can hear what I’m thinking, at least not in that literal way. Sometimes I worry people can tell I’m acting weird because of how I look or what I’m doing, but that’s more from me noticing their faces or them reacting, not because I think my thoughts are being broadcast like a radio or something.\n\nSo… no, not really. Not that I’ve noticed. If you mean something more subtle than that, I might need you to explain it, but the way you said it, I don’t think that’s happened to me.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or arranging the boxes, do you ever have a moment where you know you don’t want to do it but feel like you can’t stop it from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there is sometimes a moment like that, but it’s very small, like I don’t get enough time to do anything with it.\n\nLike at the warehouse, with the forklift, if it starts backing up and the beeping is going, I can sort of know, “Don’t do it, don’t get stuck here,” because I’ve already gotten in trouble for it. I know people are trying to work and I know it’s dangerous or annoying or whatever. But then I’ll freeze there with my arms up, or I’ll start copying the beeping back, and by the time I’m aware enough to think, “Stop, stop doing that,” it’s already happening.\n\nIt’s the same with the hand signals. Someone gives a signal and I’ll copy it, even if part of me is embarrassed and knows they’re going to yell. Or with the boxes, I don’t set out thinking, “I’m going to make these weird patterns and hold everyone up.” I just get pulled into moving them around into these little arrangements, and then I hear people getting mad, and that makes me realize I’m doing it again.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do sometimes know I don’t want to, but it doesn’t feel like that knowing is enough to stop it right then. It feels like I’m already caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a brief moment before you start doing these things—like copying sounds, gestures, or moving boxes—where you have a clear feeling that it’s about to happen, even if you can’t stop it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I think there is a tiny moment, but it’s not clear like a warning bell. It’s more like I suddenly realize, “Oh no, this is the thing again,” and then it’s already happening.\n\nLike with the forklift at the warehouse, if it starts beeping or someone is giving hand signals, I’ll sort of feel myself getting caught on it. I might freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sound, or I copy the gesture, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m in the way or I’m making it worse. But the “about to happen” feeling is so short that I don’t really get to choose. It’s like by the time I notice, my body is already doing it.\n\nAnd with the boxes, it’s similar. I don’t plan it out ahead of time. I just see them sitting there, and then I’m moving them into these weird little patterns, like they need to be arranged a certain way, even though everyone else just wants me to keep working. I guess that’s part of why they brought me in, because I keep doing that stuff even when people are upset and telling me to stop.\n\nSo… yes, maybe there’s a brief moment, but it’s not useful. It doesn’t feel like enough time to stop myself.\n\nDoctor: After you finish doing the movements, copying the sounds, or arranging the boxes, do you ever feel a sense of relief, satisfaction, or like something is settled, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it relief exactly. It’s not like I do the thing and then feel calm in a good way, like, “Okay, that fixed it.” Sometimes there’s maybe a tiny feeling that it’s… finished, or like my body stops pulling at me for a second, but mostly I just notice everyone reacting.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I copy the beeping back, or copy the hand signals, I’m not doing it because I think, “This will feel satisfying.” It just happens, and then after it’s done I’m usually aware that people are yelling or staring, and I feel stupid or embarrassed or scared I’m going to get in more trouble.\n\nWith the boxes, maybe there’s a little more of that settled feeling, because when I move them into those strange little patterns, part of me feels like they’re in the right place for a moment. But then someone comes over and says, “What are you doing, stop messing with those,” and then that feeling goes away and I just feel caught, like I did it again and I don’t really know how to explain why.\n\nSo… maybe for the boxes, yes, a small moment of “that’s how it should be,” but with the sounds and movements it’s more like it ends and then I have to deal with everyone being mad.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements, copying sounds, or arranging the boxes, do you ever feel a subtle inner pressure, discomfort, or need building up inside that makes you feel like you have to do it, even if only for a moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there is something like that, but it’s hard to separate it out from the thing itself. It’s not like I’m standing there calmly and then I feel this big pressure building for a long time. It’s more like I’m at the warehouse and something catches me, like the forklift starts beeping, or somebody makes one of those hand signals, and suddenly there’s this tight feeling, like my body has already decided before I have.\n\nThat’s why I guess they brought me in, because I keep doing these things even when it makes no sense. I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I’ll copy the beeping sounds back, or copy the hand signals, and people are yelling, like, “Move, stop doing that,” and I know I’m causing a problem. But in that moment it feels like there’s this need or pull, not exactly in words, just like I have to follow it.\n\nWith the boxes it’s maybe easier to notice. I’ll see them sitting there and then there’s this uncomfortable feeling that they’re not right, like they need to be moved into these little patterns. So I start arranging them, even if everyone is trying to work around me and getting mad. I know it looks strange. I know it holds things up. But it’s like the pressure is there and I’m already doing it before I can talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the movements you make—like raising your arms or copying gestures—are completely out of your control, like your body is moving on its own without you meaning to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot of the time it does feel like that. Not like I black out or anything, I’m there, I can see what’s happening, but it’s like my body gets ahead of me.\n\nLike at the warehouse, by the forklift, I’ll suddenly be standing there with my arms up, frozen in that position, or I’ll start doing the same hand signal somebody else is doing. And I know it looks ridiculous or dangerous, because people are yelling at me to move or stop, but in that moment it doesn’t feel like I sat there and chose, “Okay, now I’m going to raise my arms.” It feels like it just… starts.\n\nSame with the beeping sounds. The forklift beeps, and then I’m copying it back before I really mean to. I hear myself doing it, and then I realize people are staring or getting mad. It’s embarrassing because I’m not trying to mess around or be funny. I know it causes problems.\n\nAnd with the boxes, I guess that’s a little different because it’s more like I get stuck moving them into these weird little patterns. But even that doesn’t feel fully under my control. I’ll know I’m supposed to be working normally, and people are telling me to stop, but I keep doing it anyway, like my hands are already going.\n\nSo, yeah… I’d say it feels out of my control, or mostly out of my control. I’m aware of it, but I don’t feel like I’m the one steering it very well.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not really sure. I don’t think I have, like… a regular thing where I’m blinking over and over or shrugging or making noises all day, if that’s what you mean.

But when I’m just sitting somewhere or walking around outside of work, I haven’t really noticed sudden little movements like that. I don’t really feel like I’m choosing it normally, if that makes sense. It’s more like I get pulled into it, and then suddenly everyone is staring.\n\nDoctor: Before you make those sounds or do those movements, do you ever feel a sort of tension or urge inside that builds up and feels like it needs to come out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it tension, exactly. Like, I don’t feel this clear thing building up in me where I’m like, “I have to make the sound now” or “I have to move my arms.” It’s not that neat.\n\nIt’s more like I’m already doing it before I really catch it. I’ll be standing near the forklift and then I’ve got my arms up, kind of stuck there, or I’m doing the beep-beep sound it makes, and I don’t remember deciding, okay, do that. Or the hand signals the other guys use, I’ll start copying them, sometimes more than once, and then someone is shouting at me like I’m messing around on purpose.\n\nWith the boxes, too, it’s like I get caught up putting them in these little patterns. I know they’re supposed to go where the freight goes, and people are yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, but in the moment it just feels like that’s what I’m doing, like I’m locked into it. So… no, I don’t really notice a build-up first. It’s more like I realize after I’m already in it.\n\nDoctor: When you’re doing these movements or arranging the boxes in patterns, do you feel like your thoughts are controlling what you’re doing, or does it feel like something outside of you is making you do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think of it like, “something outside is making me,” exactly. It’s not like I see someone pushing my arms or anything. But it also doesn’t feel like normal thoughts, like I’m planning it out.\n\nAt the warehouse it’s more like I just get caught in it. I’ll be near the forklift and then I’m just standing there with my arms up, sort of frozen, and I didn’t really decide, “Okay, put your arms up now.” Or I hear the forklift beeping and then I’m doing the same beep-beep thing back, like copying it, and then I keep doing it until someone snaps at me. Same with the hand signals the guys use — I’ll repeat them, like my hands just go into it.\n\nAnd the boxes… that’s the part they get the most mad about, because I start moving them into these little patterns, like they line up a certain way in my head, but I know that’s not how we’re supposed to stack freight. People are yelling, “Stop doing that, you’re holding us up,” and I’m trying to stop, but I’m already in the middle of it.\n\nSo… I guess it feels like it’s coming from me, but not in a way I’m fully choosing. Not like a clear outside force, more like I’m pulled into it and then I realize everyone is staring.\n\nDoctor: Do these movements, sounds, or patterns happen only at work, or do they also happen when you're at home or in other places?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work, yeah. I mean, that’s where people notice it and where it gets me in trouble, because everything is moving fast there and I’m supposed to be stacking freight or moving boxes, not… standing there.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and I sort of freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping sound it makes — that little backing-up beep — and I’ll do it over and over, or I’ll copy the hand signals the guys use. And then with the boxes, I keep moving them into these weird little patterns, like they have to sit a certain way, even though everybody’s yelling at me because I’m messing up the flow and holding things up.\n\nThat’s kind of why they brought me in, I guess. It’s not like a small private thing there. It becomes this whole scene, because I’m doing it right in the middle of work and people are telling me to stop and I’m still doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any strange thoughts, heard voices that others don’t hear, or seen things that others say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. Like… strange thoughts, everybody has strange thoughts sometimes, right? I get thoughts that feel kind of weird or hard to explain, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nAs far as voices, I don’t think I hear, like, a person standing there talking when nobody’s there. Not like in a movie or something. I’ll get caught up in noises around me, like the beeping at work, but I don’t know if that counts, because it’s a real sound first. And seeing things… I don’t think I’m seeing people or objects that other people say aren’t there.\n\nI guess I’m saying I haven’t really noticed that exactly. Mostly what I know is people tell me I’m doing things or acting weird, and I don’t always catch it until they react. But voices or seeing things that aren’t there… I’m not sure. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel a strong need or urge to do certain things over and over, even if you don’t really want to?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I’m trying to think. It’s harder to say, because at home nobody’s really watching me the same way, you know? Like if I pause or move something around, it doesn’t turn into everybody yelling.\n\nBut the big problem, the thing that made them bring me in, is definitely at the warehouse. I’ll be near the forklift and I’ll just kind of freeze there with my arms up, like I’m stuck in the middle of doing something, and I don’t really mean to stand like that. Or the forklift starts backing up and beeping, and then I catch myself making the same beep sound back, over and over. I keep moving them into these patterns, like they look wrong unless I shift them a certain way, even though I know I’m supposed to just stack them and keep things moving. People will be yelling at me to stop because I’m slowing everything down, and I still keep messing with them for a bit before I can pull myself out of it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it a “need” exactly, but yeah, it’s like I get pulled into doing things over and over even when I don’t really want to, mostly at work.\n\nDoctor: Before you start doing the movements or arranging the boxes, do you ever notice any specific feeling, sound, or thought that comes right before it happens?\n\nPatient: I don’t always catch a clear thing right before it. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, because people ask like there must be a thought first, like, “I’m going to do this now,” but it doesn’t really feel that organized to me.\n\nAt the warehouse, it’s like I’ll be near the forklift, and then suddenly I’m just… stopped, with my arms up, like I froze in the middle of something. Or the forklift starts doing that backing-up beep, and I notice the sound and then I’m making it too, like copying it, and I keep doing it longer than makes sense. I’m trying to check in my memory, but it’s hard because when it happens I’m usually not paying attention to my body first. I’m paying attention to the thing in front of me, or I’m already doing it before I realize.\n\nLike with the beeping, I notice the sound more than anything inside me. That’s what makes it embarrassing, honestly.
